.ufo/waves/round-79/w1/R79ConnectorA/mado-grep-move2.txt:14:corpus/QUALITY.md:403:78. A control tally is stated once, with the definition of its denominator, and every number in that sentence is recomputed from the receipt's own rows: which rows are anchors, which are controls, and which class each control is in; a second number (a member count, a verdict denominator) uses the same definition or names its own, and a breakdown that sums to the total by moving a row between classes is a defect even when the total is right. Check: `python3 -I .ufo/waves/round-43/w1/ScrQualityCriteria/check78.py --receipt <round>/receipts/ctl-c1.txt --claim <the cited line>` — it recomputes rows, anchors, controls and the `M`/`D`/`K` classes from `^ok  <NAME>` lines, compares the receipt's `controls=` header and its `0 of <n>` verdict denominator against those counts, and compares each number the claim repeats; a `TALLY_MISMATCH claim M=37 receipt=38` line and `CHECK78: verdict=FAIL` are violations. Shown able to fail: on the round-42 receipt with the round-42 report's own sentence — `46 controls (absent base, 3 do-nothing, 5 cheats, 37 mutants)` — the checker reads `rows=48 anchors=2 controls=46 M=38 D=3 K=5 header=46 verdict_of=47` and `TALLY_MISMATCH claim M=37 receipt=38`; the header's `controls=46` is `len(jobs) - 2` (`run-c1-controls.py:139`), the verdict's `0 of 47` is `len(jobs) - 1`, and the claim's own breakdown is `1+3+5+37`, so the report's 46 includes the base anchor and loses one mutant. The repaired form passes: the round-43 registration states `48 `ok` lines … 46 controls: 3 do-nothing, 5 cheats, 38 single-defect mutants` and `CHECK78: verdict=OK` (`receipts-checkers.txt`). Mistake class: denominator drift — a right total under the wrong membership, which no reader can detect without recounting. (lesson: `lessons/round-42-verifier-spotcheck.md:85-86` §6.3, which already names `run-c1-controls.py:139`)

[col 497]\n 184│\n*185│## Round24 continuation — class witnesses, error boundaries and pairing scope\n 186│\n 187│All standing criteria, including KQ20 and KQ25, remain binding. KQ35 qualifies KQ34; this continuation adds no criterion and supersedes none.\n 188│\n*189│- **Paired obligations require substantive, class-specific witnesses.** A declared blanket-refusal operator must turn a correct normal verdict (exit 0 or 1) on an audit-sufficient control into refusal (exit 2), with its named reason. A declared unavailable-evidence acceptance operator must turn the correct content-derived refusal (exit 2) into a valid verdict (exit 0 or 1) on a genuinely unavailable required-object control. One class discharges no obligation of the other.\n 190│- **Reason loss is sensitivity, not acceptance.** KQ34\'s pinned reason remains observable: a valid, attributed same-exit refusal whose named reason changes may expose reason-loss sensitivity. It does not show that an unavailable object was accepted. An unchanged normal exit also does not, by itself, establish that the verdict document is unchanged or that a substantive exit changed; preserve its detail separately.\n 191│- **Runtime faults and invalid experiments earn no kill or obligation credit.** A traceback, internal error, timeout, harness leak, malformed verdict, invalid exit or out-of-envelope failure is could-not-evaluate, not a semantic mutant kill. Such a fault must not be re-labelled as ordinary same-exit reason-loss sensitivity. Preserve the actual class rows and unexpected rows without widening the attribution envelope.\n 192│- **Completed measurement is not admission.** Probe exit 0 records completion of a finite, named inventory on pinned inputs, not admission of this round. A valid finite survivor remains a completed measurement. Report each survivor with its reason; an undocumented survivor discharges no obligation.\n 193│- **No trade is waived by disclosure.** `AXES.md` §Working the axes (fleet root, lines 34–36) still requires every claim to verify and no axis to be harmed. Losing a correct runnable workflow is not an admissible trade merely because it is disclosed.\n*194│- **Original and prospective expectations remain distinct.** The original `preregistration.json` `expected_after[3]` is withdrawn/not-achieved, never retroactively passed. `oracle-amendment-1.json` is the prospective correction. .ufo/waves/round-80/w1/R80ConnectorA/mado--corpus__QUALITY.md.blob:377:65. A counter that proves a tool converts or compares draws its fixtures from a lattice on the tree index, so every spelling class the host really carries appears in every run whatever the random draw gives, and its control list carries one single-defect mutant per class; a mutant that survives is a missing class, not noise. Shown able to fail: the v1 counter left 14 of 22 further mutants at 13/13 (`.ufo/waves/round-40/w1/MutC1Controls.md`); the v2 harness scores 57/57 controls below target (`.ufo/waves/round-40/receipts/ctl-c1-v2.txt`). (lesson: lessons/round-40.md#mistakes M40-1)
.ufo/waves/round-80/w1/R80ConnectorA/mado--corpus__QUALITY.md.blob:403:78. A control tally is stated once, with the definition of its denominator, and every number in that sentence is recomputed from the receipt's own rows: which rows are anchors, which are controls, and which class each control is in; a second number (a member count, a verdict denominator) uses the same definition or names its own, and a breakdown that sums to the total by moving a row between classes is a defect even when the total is right. Check: `python3 -I .ufo/waves/round-43/w1/ScrQualityCriteria/check78.py --receipt <round>/receipts/ctl-c1.txt --claim <the cited line>` — it recomputes rows, anchors, controls and the `M`/`D`/`K` classes from `^ok  <NAME>` lines, compares the receipt's `controls=` header and its `0 of <n>` verdict denominator against those counts, and compares each number the claim repeats; a `TALLY_MISMATCH claim M=37 receipt=38` line and `CHECK78: verdict=FAIL` are violations. Shown able to fail: on the round-42 receipt with the round-42 report's own sentence — `46 controls (absent base, 3 do-nothing, 5 cheats, 37 mutants)` — the checker reads `rows=48 anchors=2 controls=46 M=38 D=3 K=5 header=46 verdict_of=47` and `TALLY_MISMATCH claim M=37 receipt=38`; the header's `controls=46` is `len(jobs) - 2` (`run-c1-controls.py:139`), the verdict's `0 of 47` is `len(jobs) - 1`, and the claim's own breakdown is `1+3+5+37`, so the report's 46 includes the base anchor and loses one mutant. The repaired form passes: the round-43 registration states `48 `ok` lines … 46 controls: 3 do-nothing, 5 cheats, 38 single-defect mutants` and `CHECK78: verdict=OK` (`receipts-checkers.txt`). Mistake class: denominator drift — a right total under the wrong membership, which no reader can detect without recounting. (lesson: `lessons/round-42-verifier-spotcheck.md:85-86` §6.3, which already names `run-c1-controls.py:139`)
